ASYMMETRY, stated explicitly: the locale toggle triggers a FULL server round-trip — next-intl remounts the whole `[locale]` subtree under the other prefix (new RSC request, new message catalog, new `<html lang/dir>`), whereas the theme toggle (`AppChrome` L89 `setTheme`) is next-themes' pure-client `.dark`/`.light` class swap on `<html>` with ZERO network. PostgREST offers no client-side multi-table transaction, so I evaluated (a) sequential authenticated-client writes + compensating cleanup vs (b) one `SECURITY DEFINER` rpc. **Chose (b-INVOKER): one atomic `SECURITY INVOKER` rpc `betk.submit_seller_application(...)`.** (a) rejected — `seller_profiles` has no DELETE policy so compensation needs a service-role reach-around AND is still non-atomic (a crash mid-sequence strands rows). (b-DEFINER) rejected — `SECURITY DEFINER` bypasses RLS, so the RESTRICTIVE `seller_profiles_phone_gate` (OD-4 / REG-10) would NOT bite (would need a hand-rolled `phone_number IS NOT NULL` check — a silent-defeat risk), AND it would add advisor **0029** (`authenticated_security_definer_function_executable`), violating the "no new advisor findings" bar. **(b-INVOKER)** gets atomicity (PostgREST wraps each rpc in a transaction → the 4-row write commits or rolls back as one → the slug-collision test proves ZERO residue) WITHOUT bypassing RLS: the phone gate bites naturally on the first INSERT (REG-10 honored at the DB layer, no hand-rolled check), `sp_insert`/`stores_insert`/`sdoc_own` enforce `id`/`seller_id = auth.uid()`, and the function `SET search_path = betk, public` + `REVOKE … FROM PUBLIC` + `GRANT EXECUTE … TO authenticated` keeps it out of advisors 0011/0028/0029. **The role flip stays OUT of the rpc and runs LAST** — `betk.users` has no permissive UPDATE policy (REG-19 / ADR-010), so a `SECURITY INVOKER` fn can't touch it anyway; the action calls the new column-scoped service-role helper `setUserRole(uid,'seller')` AFTER the rpc commits, so the `seller_profiles` row provably exists before the flip (satisfies the ordering requirement; Binding rules held: no `loading.tsx` wrapping `notFound()`-capable segments; zero `components/ui/*`/`shared/*` internal edits. **REG-28 INVESTIGATE:** probed prod (`next start`) — every unknown route + every explicit `notFound()` returned Next's bare `<html id="__next_error__">` shell (no `lang/dir`, no NextIntl, generic "BETK" title), confirming the drift. **ROOT CAUSE (not a specific-commit regression):** the app's ONLY `<html>`/`<body>` live in the DYNAMIC `[locale]/layout.tsx` (there is no `app/layout.tsx`); a `notFound()` bubbling out of that segment renders on the special `/_not-found` route which sits ABOVE `[locale]`, so the dynamic layout's document shell never re-applies → the bare fallback (vercel/next.js#64870, docs + issue confirm "still the case in 15.5"). Re-proved empirically: removing `[locale]/not-found.tsx` did NOT change the bare shell for bubbled `notFound()`, so the fix had to be at the routing level, not a boundary swap. BL-01-FIX's 2026-07-02 "chrome-preserving 404" runtime claim predates the drift's visibility (this is the standing framework limitation, not an intervening-track side effect). **REG-28 RESTORE:** (1) enabled `experimental.globalNotFound` in `next.config.ts`; (2) added `src/app/global-not-found.tsx` — a FULL localized HTML document (reads the negotiated locale from the `NEXT_LOCALE` cookie the middleware sets, validated vs `routing.locales`, fallback `ar`; renders `<html lang/dir>` + `NextIntlClientProvider` + kit `EmptyState` + locale-aware home link; kit components only, no bespoke 404 design); (3) added a `notFound.*` namespace to BOTH catalogs; (4) **removed `src/app/[locale]/[...rest]/page.tsx`** — it MATCHED every unknown path then threw `notFound()`, which kept `global-not-found` (fires only for genuinely-unmatched URLs) from ever engaging; with it gone, unknown paths fall through to `global-not-found`; (5) kept `[locale]/not-found.tsx` but rewrote it bilingual via `getTranslations` (was hardcoded-Arabic — incidental bug fixed) for explicit `notFound()` from matched detail pages. **STATUS-CODE TABLE (prod, re-proved):** `/xx`·`/does-not-exist`·`/en/does-not-exist`·`/xx/admin` → **404 + noindex + localized FULL document** (`ar/rtl` or `en/ltr`, `/xx/admin` no gate leak); unknown `/listing/<uuid>`·`/category/<slug>`·`/store/<slug>` (both locales) → **404 + noindex**, localized EmptyState CONTENT inside Next's default shell. **RESIDUAL (accepted, documented):** explicit `notFound()` from a MATCHED route still renders in the default shell (no `lang/dir` wrapper) — same #64870 limitation, unfixable without abandoning the unprefixed-default-locale architecture; hard-404 + noindex intact (the "must not regress" bar), and strictly better than the pre-fix all-bare state. **REG-27 GUARD:** new `scripts/check-i18n-parity.mjs` (pure-Node, built-in-only, mirrors `check-no-hardcoded-arabic.mjs` style) — deep-walks both catalogs to leaf-key dot-paths, reports totals + orphans each direction, exits 1 on any orphan OR leaf-count mismatch. - 2026-07-16 | Opus 4.8 (Cursor Agent) | R5 — promote rls-smoke on migration-touching PRs (CI-only) | **Made the `rls-smoke` job a conditionally-required, BLOCKING gate on any PR that touches `supabase/migrations/**`, while preserving its opt-in behavior everywhere else — REG-08 CLOSED. CI-only change; ZERO test-code change.** **Why:** RLS is the sole authorization boundary and the live policy set is 39 (post-T01-FIX), but the green smoke harness (`tests/integration/rls.smoke.test.ts`) only ran on `workflow_dispatch`/develop-push — never on the PRs that actually change policies. **Step 1 — workflow (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`):** extended the `rls-smoke` job's trigger `if:` to also match `github.event_name == 'pull_request'` (kept the two existing branches: `workflow_dispatch` + `push` to `develop`). GitHub has **no job-level `paths:` filter**, so instead of skipping at the job level (which posts NO status → a "required" skipped check either blocks forever or vanishes silently), the job now runs on EVERY PR and self-gates internally: (a) `checkout` uses `fetch-depth: 0` so the PR base is available to diff; (b) a new **`Detect migration changes`** step (`id: detect`) — for non-PR events sets `run=true` (explicit opt-in unchanged); for a PR runs `git diff --name-only "$BASE_SHA" "$HEAD_SHA" -- supabase/migrations/` (base/head from `github.event.pull_request.{base,head}.sha`) and, if any migration file changed, logs the changed list + sets `run=true`, else logs an explicit **"No migration changes (supabase/migrations/** untouched in this PR) — skipping RLS smoke"** line + sets `run=false`; (c) every downstream step (pnpm-setup, node-setup, cache-restore, verify-secrets, run-suite) is now guarded `if: steps.detect.outputs.run == 'true'`. Net effect: on a migration-touching PR the suite runs and can be marked *required* in branch protection → BLOCKING; on a non-migration PR the job still runs but short-circuits with a **logged no-op** (never a silent absence), so the required check always posts a status. **Step 2 — non-migration behavior preserved as an explicit log line** (the `else` branch above), not a job-level `if`-skip. **Step 3 — fail-loudly retained + strengthened:** the "Verify staging secrets are present" step still `exit 1`s when any of `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL`/`NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY`/`SUPABASE_SERVICE_KEY` is absent, with an added `::error::` line noting a migration-touching PR must be smoke-tested rather than skipped — so a migration PR from a fork (forks lack secrets) fails VISIBLY, never a silent pass. **Step 3 (cont.) — residual risk documented in the job comment:** the harness mints/tears down real staging GoTrue auth users, so concurrent runs could collide on auth users; mitigations recorded = (a) the harness's per-run unique-suffix on unique columns + prefix-sweep of crashed-run leftovers (confirmed by reading `tests/integration/rls.smoke.test.ts` header: "unique string columns carry a per-run suffix; a prefix sweep removes any crashed-run leftovers before seeding"), (b) the existing `concurrency: ci-${{ github.ref }}` + `cancel-in-progress` (a PR never races itself; cross-PR races covered by (a)). Also rewrote the top-of-job comment block + the file-header comment to describe the new conditional-required model; renamed the job `RLS smoke (opt-in, staging)` → `RLS smoke (staging)`. **Step 4 — docs:** `docs/08-deployment/CICD_PIPELINE.md` gained a dedicated "RLS smoke (conditionally required)" bullet + a cross-reference on the Migrations bullet; SESSION_CONTEXT REG-08 flipped to ✅ Closed + a new "Last completed" R5 entry; this journal entry. **NOT run this task** — per the explicit instruction, the next migration-touching PR proves the gate. **Verify:** `python -c "import yaml; yaml.safe_load(...)"` on `ci.yml` → parses clean (`YAML OK`); no `src/` or test file changed → typecheck/guards/build unaffected (not re-run, nothing to change). **Issues found:** none. **Tests added:** none (CI-only, no test-code change, per scope). **Docs updated:** `docs/08-deployment/CICD_PIPELINE.md`, `SESSION_CONTEXT.md`, this journal. **Files changed:** `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `docs/08-deployment/CICD_PIPELINE.md`, `docs/10-ai-development/SESSION_CONTEXT.md`, this journal. **Next: unchanged — the DS token-apply/regeneration + the standing HOLDs before Phase 03 / T02; the branch-protection owner should now mark `RLS smoke (staging)` as a required status check so the gate becomes enforced.**

Enums checked: `order_status` includes `confirmed`; `listing_status` includes `sold_out`; `order_items.quantity SMALLINT`; `listings.stock_qty INTEGER` nullable, `CHECK (stock_qty >= 0)`. **No STOP-flag** — the 4 enumerated potential ambiguities (transition, per-qty, sold_out flip, behavior at 0) are all specified; the remaining edges are deterministic from existing constraints (documented in-line, not invented). **Implementation:** `betk.decrement_stock_on_confirm()` `LANGUAGE plpgsql SECURITY DEFINER SET search_path = betk, public`; body does ONE `UPDATE betk.listings … FROM (SELECT listing_id, SUM(quantity) qty FROM betk.order_items WHERE order_id=NEW.id GROUP BY listing_id) oi WHERE l.id=oi.listing_id AND l.stock_qty IS NOT NULL`, setting `stock_qty = stock_qty - qty`, `status = CASE WHEN stock_qty-qty=0 AND status='active' THEN 'sold_out' ELSE status END`, `updated_at=NOW()`. Trigger `trg_decrement_stock_on_confirm AFTER UPDATE OF status ON betk.orders FOR EACH ROW WHEN (OLD.status IS DISTINCT FROM NEW.status AND NEW.status='confirmed')`. Edge decisions grounded in schema/rules: untracked NULL stock skipped; oversell → existing `CHECK` rolls the confirm back (no clamp); only `active`→`sold_out` (R-L06/R-L07 machine); fires once on the INTO-confirmed transition. **Apply + ledger discipline (R1 lesson):** MCP `apply_migration` generates its OWN version timestamp, so applied FIRST then named the local files to the recorded versions — `20260716124323_decrement_stock_on_confirm.sql` + `20260716125122_harden_decrement_stock_fn_execute.sql`; re-read `schema_migrations` (17 rows) and diffed `ls supabase/migrations` → **17/17 1:1, zero orphans**, no version↔filename drift. **Security hardening (advisor-driven):** initial apply left the SECURITY DEFINER fn with the default `PUBLIC` EXECUTE grant → `get_advisors` flagged NEW lints **0028 (anon)** + **0029 (authenticated)** `..._security_definer_function_executable` ("callable via /rest/v1/rpc") — a trigger-returning fn IS still statically flagged. Fixed with the 2nd migration: `REVOKE EXECUTE ON FUNCTION betk.decrement_stock_on_confirm() FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated;` (advisor's own remediation; triggers fire regardless of EXECUTE privilege). partial→decrement-by-qty, stays active; multi-item→per-listing qty; NULL→unchanged, never sold_out; fires-once (confirmed→preparing no re-decrement); oversell→CHECK error + rollback (stock unchanged, order stays pending). **6/6 PASS**, twice (before + after the revoke — proving the revoke doesn't break firing); - 2026-07-02 | Sonnet 5 (Cursor Agent) | OD-7 / BL-03 | **Settings language + theme switcher — DONE, NOT yet committed.** Added the Account → Settings section (no new route — the frozen UI Spec has one `/account` page; "Account → Settings" is a section within it) with a language switch (AR↔EN) and a theme switch (light/dark/system), composed entirely from the two already-installed OD-7 libraries (`next-intl` navigation + `next-themes`) — no new dependency, no DB column, no restyle of `components/ui`/`components/shared`. **New `src/app/[locale]/(buyer)/account/_components/LanguageSwitcher.tsx`** ("use client") — reads the current locale via `useLocale()`, the canonical (locale-stripped) path via `usePathname()` from `@/i18n/navigation`, and on change calls `router.replace(pathname, { locale: nextLocale })` (same `@/i18n/navigation` `useRouter`) — this is next-intl's own locale-aware router, so it adds/removes the `/en` prefix for the SAME page and the next-intl middleware (BL-01) sets the `NEXT_LOCALE` cookie on the resulting request; a no-op guard skips navigation if the selected value equals the current locale. **New `src/app/[locale]/(buyer)/account/_components/ThemeSwitcher.tsx`** ("use client") — thin wrapper over `next-themes`' `useTheme()`; a `mounted` boolean (set in `useEffect`) gates the rendered `value`/`disabled` state so the control never reads the client-only `theme` before hydration (avoids a hydration-mismatch warning on THIS control — the page-wide anti-flash guarantee is next-themes' injected script + `suppressHydrationWarning` on `<html>`, already shipped in BL-01); `onChange` calls `setTheme()` directly, so persistence is next-themes' own `localStorage`, per OD-7 (no DB column). Both components follow the exact unstyled `data-slot`/`<label htmlFor>`/plain `<select>` pattern already used by `ProfileEditForm`/`DeactivateAccountForm` (still `TODO(Phase DS)`, not restyled here) for visual consistency pending the Claude-Design catalog kit. **Wired into `src/app/[locale]/(buyer)/account/page.tsx`** — a new `<section data-slot="account-settings">` (heading + both switchers) inserted between the profile-edit section and the deactivate-account section. **`messages/{ar,en}.json`** — new `account.settings` namespace (`sectionLabel`, `title`, `languageLabel`, `languageOptions.{ar,en}`, `themeLabel`, `themeOptions.{light,dark,system}`); the two language-option labels are intentionally the SAME literal in both catalogs (`"العربية"` / `"English"`) — these are proper nouns naming the target language in its own script, a deliberate i18n choice (common language-picker convention), not a missed translation; this is JSON DATA, not `.tsx` source, so it's outside `check-no-hardcoded-arabic`'s scan surface (which only walks `.ts`/`.tsx`). **VERIFY:** `pnpm typecheck` clean (exit 0); `pnpm lint` clean (only the pre-existing unrelated `no-img-element`×3 / `no-console`×10 warnings, none in touched files); all 3 guards green (`check-no-hardcoded-arabic` 29 files — up from 27, the two new component files added zero violations; `check-service-import`; `check-zod-coverage` 7 files, unaffected — no new Server Actions); `/en/account` → 200 at `/en/auth/login?returnUrl=/account` — the buyer-gate gate verdict + locale-preserving redirect are byte-identical to pre-BL-03 (proves the new client components didn't regress the page's compile/gate behavior). **Not independently runtime-verified via an authenticated browser session** (would require minting a real GoTrue session + replicating `@supabase/ssr`'s cookie encoding, or a Playwright browser — `pnpm exec playwright install chromium` could not download a browser binary in this sandbox, no network egress for the install); the interactive behavior (does the `<select>` onChange actually flip the theme/locale) rests on (a) the library APIs being used exactly per their TypeScript signatures (checked directly against `node_modules/next-intl`'s `.d.ts` for `useRouter().replace(href, {locale})` and against `next-themes`' documented `useTheme()`/`setTheme` contract — both are the same two libraries BL-01 already proved live via a real Playwright dark-mode check on `<html class>`) and (b) the identical unstyled-`<select>`-plus-`useActionState`/`useTranslations` pattern already shipping unverified-by-e2e in `ProfileEditForm`/`DeactivateAccountForm` since Phase 02 T05/T06 — same verification depth as those, flagged here for transparency rather than silently assumed complete. **Issues found:** none new. **Tests added:** none (pure client-side UI wiring over two already-proven libraries; The authoritative SQL contract (`BETK_DATABASE_SCHEMA.sql`) `ENABLE`d RLS on them but OMITTED the matching `CREATE POLICY` statements, so the verbatim Phase-01 migration (`…_functions_rls.sql`) left them RLS-enabled-no-policy → default-deny (the exact tables behind Phase-01 T08 finding F1's "~21 RLS-enabled-no-policy ... arrive in later phases"). So this completes planned Phase-01 RLS; it does NOT invent access. **Live state confirmed BEFORE writing** (MCP `pg_policies`, read-only): all 5 RLS-enabled with **0** policies; parents `listings_public`/`reviews_public`/`collections_public` confirmed live (the EXISTS subqueries below run under the caller's RLS on the parent, so they resolve for anon precisely because those parent public policies exist). **Migration (ONE file, ADDITIVE only — no table/column/grant change, no service-role, no touching existing policies):** `supabase/migrations/20260701021800_catalog_public_read_rls.sql` adds five PERMISSIVE FOR SELECT policies, each scoped to publicly-visible PARENT rows only (deliberately NOT mirroring the owner/admin branches of the parent — those child-row reads belong to their owning phases and will OR-combine additively): `listing_images_public` = `EXISTS(listings l WHERE l.id=listing_images.listing_id AND l.status='active' AND l.deleted_at IS NULL)`; `listing_tags_public` = same active+not-deleted listing scope; `review_photos_public` = `EXISTS(reviews r WHERE r.id=review_photos.review_id AND r.is_visible=TRUE)`; `collection_listings_public` = `EXISTS(collections c WHERE c.id=collection_listings.collection_id AND c.status='live')`; `rating_aggregates_public` = `USING (TRUE)` — JUSTIFIED: the table holds ONLY pre-aggregated per-store metrics (store_id + average_rating + total_reviews + rating_1..5 + last_recalculated_at), NO PII / no row-level user data, and the ERD §3 specs it "public"/"read-public". - 2026-06-30 | Opus 4.8 (Cursor Agent) | Phase 02 / T08-FIX | **OTP ≤5-attempt limiter re-anchored to the OTP lifecycle — open-issue #12 (T08 hard failure) RESOLVED.** **Root cause (T08):** `src/services/otpLimiter.ts` keyed `attempt_count` on an ABSOLUTE wall-clock 60s epoch bucket (`challengeWindowStart = Math.floor(Date.now()/60000)*60000`, `token_hash = sha256("betk-otp-challenge:{phone}:{windowStartMs}")`) computed at *verify* time, unrelated to OTP issuance. A mid-bucket 60s OTP straddled the next epoch boundary → a SECOND `otp_tokens` row with a fresh 1–5 counter → ~10 attempts on one valid code; `chk_otp_attempts CHECK(<=5)` caps each row but not the pair; GoTrue has no per-token verify counter (ADR-010 §4) so this app counter is the sole owner of AC-AUTH-2 clause 4. **Fix — anchor the counter to the OTP, not the clock:** REMOVED `challengeWindowStart` + the window-derived hash. ADDED `createOtpChallenge(phone)`, called at SEND time in `sendOtp` + `sendPhoneOtp` AFTER a successful GoTrue send (placement matters: a rate-limited resend must NOT supersede a still-valid challenge) — it supersedes any prior active row for the phone (`update is_used=true where is_used=false and expires_at>now`; deterministic OVERLAPPING-ROWS rule = freshest-wins, with `SMS_MAX_FREQUENCY=60s` normally preventing overlap) then inserts ONE row (`expires_at = now + 60s`, `attempt_count = 0`, `is_used = false`, `token_hash = randomBytes(32).toString("hex")` — an opaque server nonce, NEVER the OTP, and no longer used for lookup; retained only for the NOT NULL column + audit id). REWORKED `recordOtpAttempt(phone)` (still called BEFORE the GoTrue verify so a wrong/expired code still counts): selects the MOST-RECENT active row (`phone_number=phone AND is_used=false AND expires_at>now`, `order expires_at desc limit 1`) and increments THAT row; no active row → `{allowed:false, attemptsUsed:0}`; `attempt_count >= MAX(5)` → reject; the DB `chk_otp_attempts CHECK(<=5)` stays the hard backstop (logic stops at 5 before writing). `markOtpUsed` now closes the active row(s) on success. The SAME row serves the entire 60s OTP lifetime, so crossing a 60s epoch boundary can no longer reset the counter. **No-leak preserved (AC-AUTH-2):** the limiter only ever receives the phone + attempt counts — never the OTP; nothing logged/persisted/returned/Sentry. **Fail-closed:** if `createOtpChallenge` throws after the SMS is sent, the send action returns a generic Arabic error (verify would reject without an active row anyway). **Both verify paths covered (shared limiter, reuse-not-fork):** sign-in `verifyOtp` and phone-capture `verifyPhoneOtp` drive the same `createOtpChallenge`/`recordOtpAttempt`/`markOtpUsed`. **Regression test (must FAIL on old, PASS on fix) — `tests/unit/otpLimiter.straddle.test.ts` (new):** mocks `@/lib/supabase/service` with an in-memory `otp_tokens` store + `vi.useFakeTimers`; issues an OTP at (epoch boundary − 30s), drives 5 attempts before the boundary and a 6th after it (boundary+1s, still < issued+60s so the OTP is valid), asserts the 6th is REJECTED. Confirmed RED on the pre-fix code (observed `expected true to be false` — 6th wrongly allowed) and GREEN on the fix; plus lifecycle (open→5→6th-blocked), no-challenge-reject, resend-supersede (exactly one active row), expiry-reject, and markOtpUsed-closes coverage (8 tests). **Issues found:** none new. **Tests added:** `otpLimiter.straddle.test.ts` (8 unit); extended `tests/integration/auth.otp.test.ts` limiter block to the send→verify→used contract (+supersede/no-challenge/markOtpUsed cases) and `tests/unit/auth.unit.test.ts` §4 to assert the opaque random nonce (removed the obsolete window-derived-hash assertions). **Docs updated:** SESSION_CONTEXT (project state, Last completed, open-issue #12 → RESOLVED, Phase-03 checklist blocker cleared, Next task → T08 re-run + sign-off), this journal. **CI: pnpm typecheck clean (exit 0); pnpm lint clean (pre-existing T11 service console warnings only); check-service-import clean; check-zod-coverage clean (7 files); pnpm test:unit 40/40; auth.otp integration 9/9 PASS against staging (1 manual skipped).** **Files changed:** `src/services/otpLimiter.ts`, `src/features/auth/actions/sendOtp.ts`, `src/features/auth/actions/sendPhoneOtp.ts`, `tests/unit/otpLimiter.straddle.test.ts` (new), `tests/integration/auth.otp.test.ts`, `tests/unit/auth.unit.test.ts`. **Next: T08 RE-RUN — exit verification + Phase 02 sign-off (do NOT re-run sign-off in this window; do NOT start Phase 03).**

(2) "expired/used rejected" ✅ GoTrue single-use + `isOtpExpired`; expiry=60s (R-A02) via `SMS_OTP_EXP` default 60, pinned on hosted dashboard; "one active/60s" via `SMS_MAX_FREQUENCY=60s`. SMS OTP length(6)/expiry(60s) are GoTrue defaults (not first-class config.toml keys in this CLI) → pin on the hosted dashboard. **Staging is HOSTED → provider enablement is a dashboard action** (no MCP auth-config tool exists; no Mgmt-API token in env; the plain `supabase` binary's db path doesn't push hosted auth config). **Human runbook before T02 live test:** Authentication → Providers → enable Phone (Twilio secret) + Google (Client ID/Secret); set OTP length=6, expiry=60; URL Configuration → add `https://<staging>/auth/callback`; Google Cloud Console authorized redirect URI = `https://sojmjvohiziapiwkzsjg.supabase.co/auth/v1/callback`. **PRIMITIVE (step 3):** `src/validations/auth.ts` (`authIdentitySchema` — id uuid, phoneNumber nullable ≤15, authProvider enum); `src/services/authUsers.ts` (server-only; service-role `getUserRowById` + `insertUserRow` with 23505 race/phone-collision discrimination) — placed in `src/services/` because **`betk.users` has ONLY `users_self (SELECT)`, no permissive INSERT/UPDATE** (confirmed in `…_functions_rls.sql`; ERD §3 "INSERT=(Supabase Auth)"), so the CREATE branch needs the service-role path and the `check-service-import` guard forbids that client in `src/features`; `src/features/auth/queries/findOrCreateUser.ts` (Zod-validates identity, finds by id or creates, **re-checks R-A05** `status='active' AND deleted_at IS NULL` → typed `UserDeactivatedError`/`UserNotActiveError`/`PhoneNumberTakenError` so a deactivated user can't be resurrected via OAuth in T03; does NOT set last_login_at).
